I work with camera cards and edits, and I’ll label a card wrong, miss a timecode note, or export a client cut with the wrong lower-third—even after I thought I checked it. It’s been happening enough that it scared me a bit.\n\nDoctor: Are you noticing these kinds of mistakes or difficulties in other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the work stuff that’s been really obvious. That’s what scared me into coming in—the technical mistakes I know I shouldn’t be making. Like I’ll label a camera card wrong, miss a timecode note, or export something with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I checked it. I’m not really sure how much I’m noticing it outside of work, but at work it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things that you normally wouldn't forget?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a big pattern of forgetting things in general. It’s more that I realize after the fact that I missed something I thought I’d handled.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or stressed about making these mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I’m worried about it in the moment, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious overall. It’s more like… I catch a mistake or someone points one out, and then I feel embarrassed and tense because I know I should’ve caught it. But I haven’t really thought about whether that’s stress or just frustration.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work or thinking about these mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. If I’m away from work and not thinking about the mistakes, I don’t usually feel anxious. It’s more tied to when something happens or I’m worried I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I get frustrated or embarrassed sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I feel sad or down most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s a big part of why I came in. I’m making these dumb technical mistakes at work that I know better than to make—like labeling camera cards wrong, missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third, even after I thought I checked it. It feels like I’m not staying locked in on the details.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: Not especially. I mean, I get tired after work like anyone, but I haven’t noticed feeling unusually tired lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there tasks or situations at work that you've been avoiding because of these mistakes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m avoiding tasks exactly. I mean, I do get nervous about the detailed stuff now, but I can’t think of a specific work situation I’ve been actively staying away from because of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s more that I realize after the fact that I missed something I thought I’d handled.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or stressed about making these mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I’m worried about it in the moment, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious overall. It’s more like… I catch a mistake or someone points one out, and then I feel embarrassed and tense because I know I should’ve caught it. But I haven’t really thought about whether that’s stress or just frustration.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work or thinking about these mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. If I’m away from work and not thinking about the mistakes, I don’t usually feel anxious. It’s more tied to when something happens or I’m worried I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I get frustrated or embarrassed sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I feel sad or down most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s a big part of why I came in. I’m making these dumb technical mistakes at work that I know better than to make—like labeling camera cards wrong, missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third, even after I thought I checked it. It feels like I’m not staying locked in on the details.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: Not especially. I mean, I get tired after work like anyone, but I haven’t noticed feeling unusually tired lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there tasks or situations at work that you've been avoiding because of these mistakes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m avoiding tasks exactly. I mean, I do get nervous about the detailed stuff now, but I can’t think of a specific work situation I’ve been actively staying away from because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed or upset by things that aren't related to your work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have normal life stress, but I haven’t noticed getting especially overwhelmed or upset by things outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a fog or daze?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I have moments where I’m distracted or not fully tuned in, but I wouldn’t describe it as being in a fog or a daze.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or making decisions when you're relaxed and not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed. When I’m relaxed and not thinking about work, I don’t usually have a problem making decisions or focusing in any major way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It’s more that I realize after the fact that I missed something I thought I’d handled.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or stressed about making these mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I’m worried about it in the moment, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious overall. Like I’ll label a camera card wrong, miss a timecode note, or export something with the wrong lower-third even after I thought I checked it. I’m not really sure how much I’m noticing it outside of work, but at work it’s been hard to ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things that you normally wouldn't forget?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a big pattern of forgetting things in general. It’s more that I realize after the fact that I missed something I thought I’d handled.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or stressed about making these mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I’m worried about it in the moment, but I’m not sure I’d call it anxious overall. It’s more like… I catch a mistake or someone points one out, and then I feel embarrassed and tense because I know I should’ve caught it. But I haven’t really thought about whether that’s stress or just frustration.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not at work or thinking about these mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. If I’m away from work and not thinking about the mistakes, I don’t usually feel anxious. It’s more tied to when something happens or I’m worried I missed something.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I get frustrated or embarrassed sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I feel sad or down most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s a big part of why I came in. I’m making these dumb technical mistakes at work that I know better than to make—like labeling camera cards wrong, missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third, even after I thought I checked it. It feels like I’m not staying locked in on the details.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: Not especially. I mean, I get tired after work like anyone, but I haven’t noticed feeling unusually tired lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there tasks or situations at work that you've been avoiding because of these mistakes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m avoiding tasks exactly. I mean, I do get nervous about the detailed stuff now, but I can’t think of a specific work situation I’ve been actively staying away from because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed or upset by things that aren't related to your work?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have normal life stress, but I haven’t noticed getting especially overwhelmed or upset by things outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a fog or daze?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I have moments where I’m distracted or not fully tuned in, but I wouldn’t describe it as being in a fog or a daze.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or making decisions when you're relaxed and not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed. When I’m relaxed and not thinking about work, I don’t usually have a problem making decisions or focusing in any major way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can get distracted or kind of mentally elsewhere sometimes, but I don’t feel disconnected from myself or like my surroundings aren’t real.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to things when you're doing something you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. Even when I like what I’m doing, I’ll miss details I really shouldn’t. That’s honestly what pushed me to come in — I’ve been making these dumb technical mistakes at work, like labeling camera cards wrong, missing a timecode note, or exporting a client cut with the wrong lower-third, even after I thought I checked it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed any major changes. My sleep seems about the same lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel mentally exhausted after concentrating for a while?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I don’t usually think of it as mental exhaustion specifically. If I’ve been focused for a while, I might feel kind of done with it and need a break, but I haven’t really noticed anything extreme.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or urges that you feel compelled to act on?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I can get stuck on things in my head sometimes, but not in a way where I feel like I have to do some specific action or ritual. At least nothing like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have your mistakes at work or difficulty with concentrating affected your relationships with coworkers or supervisors?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. My producers have started to trust me less on more complicated shoots. It’s not that my camera work is bad, but the mistakes or focus issues make them hesitate to give me those bigger responsibilities.\n\nAnd honestly, I feel like it’s hurting my chances of moving into a senior director of photography role. That’s been frustrating, because I know the quality of my work is there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these mistakes at work are affecting your self-esteem?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, it’s frustrating and I don’t like feeling like I’m being seen as unreliable, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s changed how I feel about myself overall. It bothers me more in the moment, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
